{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Base directory for this skill: C:\\Users\\stdray\\AppData\\Local\\Temp\\petbox-probe-ws\\.claude\\skills\\petbox-methodology\n\n# PetBox methodology — read the live rules, don't assume them\r\n\r\nPetBox lets a project turn on an optional gated process (idea → spec → work, or a custom set of\r\nboards) and pick a preset for it — `quartet`, `classic`, `simple`, a hand-tuned custom instance,\r\nor none at all. Which one (if any) `probe-m0-sandbox` runs is **not fixed by this skill** and can\r\nchange over time. **Never carry over methodology rules you learned on a different project —\r\nincluding one you may have seen documented elsewhere — they were not agreed here, and this\r\nproject's gates will reject your writes for reasons you never looked up.**\r\n\r\n## Before touching `ideas` / `spec` / `work` / `intake` boards\r\n\r\nCall the guide first, every session — rules can be edited by the project owner between sessions:\r\n\r\n```\r\ntasks_methodology_guide(projectKey:\"probe-m0-sandbox\")\r\n```\r\n\r\n(`petbox_tasks_methodology_guide` in opencode, `mcp__petbox__tasks_methodology_guide` in Claude Code.)\r\n\r\n- **No open methodology instance** → the response falls back to a generic preset baseline\r\n  (`source:\"presets\"`) purely as orientation; nothing is actually enforced. Treat the boards as\r\n  free-form until an instance exists (`tasks_methodology_create`), and don't invent gates.\r\n- **An open instance** → `markdown` is the narrative guide for THIS project right now, and\r\n  `invariants` is the same thing machine-readable. Read both before you write anything.\r\n\r\n## Reading `invariants`\r\n\r\nEach entry is `{ kind, rule, detail }`. `kind` is the board/type the rule applies to (an\r\nidea-kind invariant doesn't gate a work-kind transition, and vice versa). `rule` tells you what\r\nto check for — don't assume any of these are absent just because you haven't seen them fire yet:\r\n\r\n- `approval_gate` / `approval_gate_enforced` — **default-deny:** never set a terminal ok status\r\n  (`Done`/`accepted`-like) yourself. Exactly two exceptions, both external to you — never derive\r\n  a right to close from your own reading of `invariants`: (1) this guide states explicitly that\r\n  the kind has no approval gate (see the GATES section's \"No approval gate…\" line) — then the\r\n  executor sets the terminal status; (2) the project owner explicitly authorized it, by direct\r\n  instruction or a standing directive. Otherwise stop at the status immediately before the gated\r\n  one (e.g. `Review` before `Done`) and hand over — read it off this guide's `invariants` every\r\n  session, never from memory. `_enforced` means the server itself rejects the agent's own attempt\r\n  at the gated transition; the plain form is SOFT — the server does not block it, it holds only\r\n  because the agent honors it.\r\n- `precondition_artifact` — a transition requires a tagged comment (an \"artifact\") to already\r\n  exist on the node; `detail` names the tag.\r\n- `reason_required` — the transition call must carry a reason string.\r\n- `link_constraint` — a create or transition requires a specific link (e.g. a reference to an\r\n  accepted node on another board) or it is rejected outright.\r\n- `checklist` — preconditions to verify before attempting the transition.\r\n- `transition_effect` — a side effect fires on this transition (e.g. it auto-closes a linked\r\n  node elsewhere).\r\n- `tag_axes` — required tag-prefix axes for a kind (e.g. `area:*` / `concern:*`); tags aren't\r\n  free-form where this applies.\r\n\r\n## Tools\r\n\r\n- `tasks_methodology_guide` — call this first; resolves the project's active instance, or pass\r\n  `key` (the instance's slug) for one specific instance.\r\n- `tasks_methodology_rules_get` / `tasks_methodology_list` — the raw rules document / instance\r\n  index, for when the guide's rendering isn't enough. `_list` hands back each instance's `key`;\r\n  that same string is what every `tasks_methodology_*` verb takes as its `key`.\r\n- `tasks_methodology_get` / `tasks_search` / `tasks_node_get` / `tasks_workflow` — read\r\n  boards/nodes once you know the shape from the guide above.\r\n- `tasks_upsert` / `comments_upsert` / `relations_create` — the writes the gates above govern.\r\n\r\nAddressing: a methodology INSTANCE is addressed by `key` (its slug) on every verb — never by\r\n`name`. `name` on this surface means a document's human-readable title and addresses nothing.\r\n\r\nTool naming: base verbs are underscore-delimited (`tasks_methodology_guide`); opencode prefixes\r\n`petbox_`, Claude Code prefixes `mcp__petbox__`.\r\n\r\n## If a write gets rejected\r\n\r\nA rejection here is the methodology working, not a bug. Re-read the guide's `invariants` for the\r\n`kind`/`rule` you tripped, supply the missing link/artifact/reason, and retry. If it still\r\ndoesn't make sense after reading the guide, that itself is worth reporting — through this\r\nproject's own intake/triage path, or to its maintainer — rather than working around it.\r\n\r\n## Writing a spec node (if this project has a `spec` board)\r\n\r\n- **Format (terse-normative, EARS-lite + RFC 2119):** the node **title** is the capability; the\r\n  **body** is one normative line stating the obligation plus its condition/consequence, or\r\n  empty. Keywords: MUST / SHOULD / MAY (or this project's own language for the same three\r\n  strengths). Tag functional requirements with one axis, non-functional/invariant ones with\r\n  another — check the guide's `tag_axes` invariant for the actual prefixes this project uses.\r\n- **Altitude:** a spec node is a promise that survives reimplementation. The mechanism (data\r\n  shape, validation rules, API verbs, storage layout) is NOT a requirement — that belongs in the\r\n  work task. Test: *\"would this change if we reimplemented without changing the promise?\"* Yes →\r\n  work task; no → spec.\r\n- **Atomic but few:** one requirement per node, but at the owner altitude there are usually only\r\n  a handful — an umbrella node plus a few leaves. Don't pre-atomize implementation into the spec.\r\n\r\n## Triaging intake (if this project has an `intake` board)\r\n\r\nIntake holds raw, unrouted findings — bugs, questions, wishes — not yet placed on the pipeline.\r\nSkip it when the destination is already obvious (the report names it, or the diagnosis is clear)\r\nand create the node at the destination directly instead of parking it:\r\n- Spec-less hygiene → a work `chore` (no spec link needed).\r\n- A bug against an EXISTING spec requirement → a work `bug`, with whatever spec link this\r\n  project's `link_constraint` invariants require.\r\n- Nothing in the spec reflects the ask at all → an idea, so it goes through the idea→accept→spec\r\n  gate before any work node is opened for it.\r\nAn intake item with no matching spec never gets a shortcut straight into work — a `feature`/`bug`\r\nwork node still needs the spec link its own invariants demand.\r\n\r\n## The observations board — platform-wide, present in every project\r\n\r\n`observations` is a system-built-in board, auto-created per project, and lives outside this\r\nmethodology instance's gates (it never enters the owner's decision queue or digest). The regular\r\ntask tools apply as-is: `tasks_search`, `tasks_node_get`, `tasks_upsert`, `tasks_delta`,\r\n`comments_*`.\r\n\r\n- **Status is a value, not an FSM:** `seen` (open) → `promoted` (open) → `fixed` (terminal ok);\r\n  `declined` (terminal cancel).\r\n- **Dedup with recurrence, on every write:** a similar finding landing on this board — automatic\r\n  or a manual `tasks_upsert` — does not create a duplicate. It bumps the existing node's\r\n  `recurrenceCount`/`lastSeenAt` instead, reported back as `deduped:[{requestedKey, existingKey,\r\n  existingNodeId, recurrenceCount}]`. Only fires on a purely-creating batch (every node\r\n  `version:0`, no deletes) — don't mix creates with edits in one call.\r\n- **Promotion — `tasks_observation_promote`:** turns a `seen` observation into a real `work` task\r\n  (`type` required: `feature|bug|chore`) or an idea (`targetBoard:\"work\"|\"ideas\"`, plus\r\n  `key`/`title`/`body`/`links`/`tags`/`sessionId`). Creates an `observation_obligation` relation\r\n  (visible in `relations` from both sides) and moves the observation to `promoted` — it stays\r\n  addressable, it does not disappear.\r\n- **Fix-pinning, automatic:** the linked obligation reaching a terminal-ok status flips the\r\n  observation to `fixed` (stamps `fixedByNodeId`/`fixedAt`); a terminal-not-ok status reopens it\r\n  to `seen` — the problem wasn't fixed, it was abandoned.\r\n- **Regression detector:** a fresh hit of the same problem after a fix reopens the observation to\r\n  `seen`, stamps `recurredAfterFixAt`, surfaces it higher in search, and sets\r\n  `decisionPending:true` on the task that had \"fixed\" it — the task's own terminal status is\r\n  **not** reopened automatically, an owner call, to keep cycle-time metrics honest.\r\n  `recurrenceCount`/`lastSeenAt`/`recurredAfterFixAt`/`fixedByNodeId`/`fixedAt` all ride the\r\n  `observation` field on search/node-get hits.\r\n- A defect-like finding (broken, unexpected, contradicts docs, a process defect) goes straight\r\n  onto this board yourself — never into memory, never into a generic intake bucket. It dedups by\r\n  recurrence, so \"already observed\" is never a reason to stay silent.\r\n\r\n## Practical MCP gotchas\r\n\r\n- **Soft-delete:** `tasks_upsert` with `{key, deleted:true}` is a temporal close, history kept —\r\n  delete children first, or the whole subtree in one batch.\r\n- **`bodyLen`:** most read tools omit node bodies by default (a compact index only — identity,\r\n  status, title, tags, links). Pass `bodyLen:<N>` for a per-node body snippet (first N chars,\r\n  `…` when cut; a large N is effectively the full body).\r\n- **Response size:** a board detail call can return a large payload (tens of thousands of\r\n  characters for a busy board). Pass a high `sinceVersion`, or narrow with `underNode:<slug>` /\r\n  `groupBy`, to keep the response small; null fields are omitted from the JSON either way.\r\n"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"5b2dc129-6c34-4aae-a366-a34f9a7e0b6d","uuid":"e7ec6833-09a9-4d45-91d9-d0baabe0a3da","timestamp":"2026-09-07T08:30:22.842Z","isSynthetic":true}

This guide describes the data, it adds no rules of its own. Types, statuses, transitions and gates are ENFORCED by the server unless a rule is explicitly marked as a convention (a convention gate or a checklist is binding process, but the server does not block it).\\n\\n## Kind: classic\\n\\n- Types: task (default), feature, bug\\n- Quick-add: allowed — an untyped quick-add creates a `task`.\\n- Tags: free-form (no axes declared).\\n\\n### Workflow: task | feature | bug\\n\\n- Statuses (initial: Backlog):\\n  - open: Backlog, Todo, InProgress, Review\\n  - terminal-ok (closes the node as delivered): Done\\n  - terminal-cancel (closes the node without delivery): Cancelled, Duplicate\\n- Transitions:\\n  - Backlog -> Todo\\n  - Backlog -> InProgress\\n  - Backlog -> Review\\n  - Todo -> Backlog\\n  - Todo -> InProgress\\n  - Todo -> Review\\n  - InProgress -> Backlog\\n  - InProgress -> Todo\\n  - InProgress -> Review\\n  - Review -> Backlog\\n  - Review -> Todo\\n  - Review -> InProgress\\n  - Backlog -> Cancelled\\n  - Backlog -> Duplicate [reason required]\\n  - Todo -> Cancelled\\n  - Todo -> Duplicate [reason required]\\n  - InProgress -> Cancelled\\n  - InProgress -> Duplicate [reason required]\\n  - Review -> Cancelled\\n  - Review -> Duplicate [reason required]\\n  - Review -> Done [OWNER-ONLY]\\n  - Done -> Todo\\n  - Cancelled -> Todo\\n  - Duplicate -> Todo\\n- GATES (behavioral invariants):\\n  - Backlog -> Duplicate requires a reason: provide the `reason` field on the status-changing upsert (never the node body).\\n  - Todo -> Duplicate requires a reason: provide the `reason` field on the status-changing upsert (never the node body).\\n  - InProgress -> Duplicate requires a reason: provide the `reason` field on the status-changing upsert (never the node body).\\n  - Review -> Duplicate requires a reason: provide the `reason` field on the status-changing upsert (never the node body).\\n  - The agent NEVER performs Review -> Done — that transition is the owner's/maintainer's call, owner-only (convention — the server does not block it; this gate holds only because the agent is honest about it). Stop at Review and hand over.\\n\\n## Other kinds this server knows\\n\\n- intake, ideas, spec, work, simple — built-in preset kinds this project's methodology does NOT declare. NONE of their rules apply here and this project has no board of these kinds; nothing above and nothing in `invariants` comes from them. Listed by name only because a board of one of these kinds would still resolve (it falls back to the server preset). To read a preset's actual rules, call tasks_methodology_template_get with a builtin key (quartet|classic|simple).\\n\\n## Declaring your own kind\\n\\nThe kinds above are not the full catalog — a project can author its own: declare one with `tasks_methodology_utility_upsert` (project-homed, survives a methodology switch) or `tasks_methodology_rules_upsert` (instance-homed, goes with this instance).\\n\\n## Relation kinds\\n\\n- Structural (FSM effects and guards key on these, direction-less builtins): blocks, part_of, supersedes\\n- Neutral (free semantic edges, no process meaning): relates_to, depends_on, mirrors\\n- Declared (address via links:{kind:ref}): idea_spec (Спека реализует принятую идею — провенанс: каждый лист спеки восходит к идее, которую владелец принял.) [process; ideas → spec (реализует)], task_spec (Задача поставляет обещание спеки — feature/bug несут способность/дефект против листа спеки; chore не несёт.) [process; work → spec (поставляет)], issue_task (Задача закрывает интейк-issue — когда работа доходит до Done, входящий issue автозакрывается.) [process; intake → work (закрывает)], observation_obligation (Наблюдение промоутится в обязательство (work-фичу/баг/chore или ideas-узел), которое его адресует — наблюдение остаётся адресуемым узлом доски, а не исчезает.) Which relation kinds exist, their direction, and which are REQUIRED come from\ntasks_methodology_guide and are enforced with data-generated errors), blockedBy (the\nblocking node as a node reference — its slug key on THIS board or its 32-hex NodeId, both\naccepted, the same convention as partOf; may also be written as links.blocks, add-only),\nsupersedes (a node reference — the slug key or 32-hex NodeId, both accepted, of the node\nthis one replaces; the old one is moved to its terminal-cancel),\ncommits? (an ARRAY of commit SHAs — hex, 7..40 chars; null omits, [] clears, a list\nREPLACES the node's full commit set, same PATCH semantics as tags), decisionPending?\n(bool — \"this node is waiting on a decision from the OWNER\"; null omits, true/false sets,\na new node starts false. ORTHOGONAL to status, not a status: a node can be InProgress AND\nwaiting, and either an agent or the owner may set it. Filterable via tasks_search's\n`decisionPending`), priority? (sparse\nint, lower first), version (WATERMARK baseline: pass the\nboard `currentVersion` from your last read OR the node's own version — both are valid; 0 =\nnew; a version above this board's cursor is rejected as a wrong-scope baseline). The guard\nis about PAYLOAD, not version arithmetic: a payload identical to the node's current state\nno-ops even on an old baseline (an FSM effect or another writer already did it — no retry\nneeded), and an old baseline conflicts ONLY when the node semantically moved after your\nread — attachment writes and other bookkeeping bumps auto-resolve (their keys land in\n`autoResolved[]`). Rename via prevKey. The `board` must ALREADY exist — a cold\n\ttasks_upsert to an unknown board is REJECTED (with a \"did you mean 'X'?\" suggestion), not\n\tauto-created; create it first with tasks_board_create, or provision a methodology.\n\nTo DELETE a node, pass { key, deleted:true } (optional version baseline; 0 = delete\nregardless) — the node is soft-closed (history kept), its edges and tags are closed, and\nits key appears in `removed[]`. A node with active part_of children is refused (Rejected\nconflict) — delete the children first, or the whole subtree in one call. deleted cannot\ncombine with prevKey. Spec-node deletes need no idea_spec link (erasing junk is not a spec\nchange — retiring a real requirement stays `deprecated`).\n\nReturns the pure write-ack { applied, currentVersion, inserted, closed, conflicts[],\nadded[], updated[], removed[], autoResolved[] }. `applied` is the SINGLE source of truth:\nwhen it is FALSE\n\tnothing was written — `conflicts[]` explains every rejected key (its baseline vs the\n\tactive version, plus a reason for a guard refusal; a Stale conflict also carries\n\t`changedFields` — THIS node's payload fields that moved past your baseline, so rebase\n\ton those facts instead of blindly resubmitting) and added/updated/removed are EMPTY;\n\tre-read via tasks_delta (or tasks_search) to rebase, then resubmit. When `applied` is\n\tTRUE the echo covers ONLY this call: added/updated/removed\ncarry the call's own nodes plus nodes its cascade effects touched (a `supersedes`\ntarget obsoleted, a deleted subtree, an unblocked task) — never other writers'\nhistory, and there is no cursor parameter on a write. added/updated carry the node\n(key, nodeId, status, type, title, commits[], priority, version); `body` follows the\nuniform bodyLen knob (omitted here = NO body, a compact ack; 0 = no body; N>0 = the first\nN chars, \"…\" when cut; -1 = full body). `currentVersion` is the board-wide cursor: for a full delta\nsince a cursor (everything changed by anyone — rebase/merge/catch-up), call\ntasks_delta with it as `sinceVersion`.\n`warning` (optional) is set when an APPLIED call's request payload was large enough to\nrisk the client-side truncation described above — informational, never a refusal (the\nwrite already landed); omitted the rest of the time.\n\nON THE SYSTEM `observations` BOARD (kind `observation`), a pure-create batch (every\nnode version 0) runs a dedup-with-recurrence guard first: a node whose title+body\ntextually matches an existing observation is NOT created — the existing one's\nrecurrence counter grows instead, and `deduped[]` (requestedKey, existingKey,\nexistingNodeId, recurrenceCount) names where the write went. This guide describes the data, it adds no rules of its own. Types, statuses, transitions and gates are ENFORCED by the server unless a rule is explicitly marked as a convention (a convention gate or a checklist is binding process, but the server does not block it).\\n\\n## Kind: classic\\n\\n- Types: task (default), feature, bug\\n- Quick-add: allowed — an untyped quick-add creates a `task`.\\n- Tags: free-form (no axes declared).\\n\\n### Workflow: task | feature | bug\\n\\n- Statuses (initial: Backlog):\\n  - open: Backlog, Todo, InProgress, Review, Blocked\\n  - terminal-ok (closes the node as delivered): Done\\n  - terminal-cancel (closes the node without delivery): Cancelled, Duplicate\\n- Transitions:\\n  - Backlog -> Todo\\n  - Backlog -> InProgress\\n  - Backlog -> Review\\n  - Todo -> Backlog\\n  - Todo -> InProgress\\n  - Todo -> Review\\n  - InProgress -> Backlog\\n  - InProgress -> Todo\\n  - InProgress -> Review\\n  - Review -> Backlog\\n  - Review -> Todo\\n  - Review -> InProgress\\n  - Backlog -> Cancelled\\n  - Backlog -> Duplicate [reason required]\\n  - Todo -> Cancelled\\n  - Todo -> Duplicate [reason required]\\n  - Cancelled -> Todo\\n  - Duplicate -> Todo\\n  - Backlog -> Blocked\\n  - Todo -> Blocked\\n  - InProgress -> Blocked\\n  - Review -> Blocked\\n  - Blocked -> Backlog\\n  - Blocked -> Todo\\n  - Blocked -> InProgress\\n  - Blocked -> Review\\n  - Blocked -> Cancelled\\n  - Blocked -> Duplicate [reason required]\\n- GATES (behavioral invariants):\\n  - Backlog -> Duplicate requires a reason: provide the `reason` field on the status-changing upsert (never the node body).\\n  - Todo -> Duplicate requires a reason: provide the `reason` field on the status-changing upsert (never the node body).\\n  - InProgress -> Duplicate requires a reason: provide the `reason` field on the status-changing upsert (never the node body).\\n  - Review -> Duplicate requires a reason: provide the `reason` field on the status-changing upsert (never the node body).\\n  - The agent NEVER performs Review -> Done — that transition is the owner's/maintainer's call, owner-only (convention — the server does not block it; this gate holds only because the agent is honest about it). Stop at Review and hand over.\\n  - Blocked -> Duplicate requires a reason: provide the `reason` field on the status-changing upsert (never the node body).\\n\\n### Transition effects\\n\\nCross-node automation the SERVER executes when a node of this kind enters or leaves the trigger status — do not apply these by hand:\\n- On entering Done, outgoing `blocks` nodes currently in Blocked are set to InProgress.\\n- On entering Cancelled, outgoing `blocks` nodes currently in Blocked are set to InProgress.\\n- On entering Duplicate, outgoing `blocks` nodes currently in Blocked are set to InProgress.\\n\\n## Other kinds this server knows\\n\\n- intake, ideas, spec, work, simple — built-in preset kinds this project's methodology does NOT declare. NONE of their rules apply here and this project has no board of these kinds; nothing above and nothing in `invariants` comes from them. Listed by name only because a board of one of these kinds would still resolve (it falls back to the server preset). To read a preset's actual rules, call tasks_methodology_template_get with a builtin key (quartet|classic|simple).\\n\\n## Declaring your own kind\\n\\nThe kinds above are not the full catalog — a project can author its own: declare one with `tasks_methodology_utility_upsert` (project-homed, survives a methodology switch) or `tasks_methodology_rules_upsert` (instance-homed, goes with this instance).\\n\\n## Relation kinds\\n\\n- Structural (FSM effects and guards key on these, direction-less builtins): blocks, part_of, supersedes\\n- Neutral (free semantic edges, no process meaning): relates_to, depends_on, mirrors\\n- Declared (address via links:{kind:ref}): idea_spec (Спека реализует принятую идею — провенанс: каждый лист спеки восходит к идее, которую владелец принял.) [process; ideas → spec (реализует)], task_spec (Задача поставляет обещание спеки — feature/bug несут способность/дефект против листа спеки; chore не несёт.) [process; work → spec (поставляет)], issue_task (Задача закрывает интейк-issue — когда работа доходит до Done, входящий issue автозакрывается.) [process; intake → work (закрывает)], observation_obligation (Наблюдение промоутится в обязательство (work-фичу/баг/chore или ideas-узел), которое его адресует — наблюдение остаётся адресуемым узлом доски, а не исчезает.) open: Backlog, Todo, InProgress, Review, Blocked\n  - terminal-ok (closes the node as delivered): Done\n  - terminal-cancel (closes the node without delivery): Cancelled, Duplicate\n- Transitions:\n  -
